> **🚨 `opus-completeness-crisis-2026-06-05` — 2026-06-05 — "PRODUCT-GRADE" ≠ COMPLETE (the metric's blind spot) + 2 UX fixes**. John pressed on the "product-grade" claim and exposed a real, vault-wide problem: **the document scorecard grades node QUALITY (wedge/dates/refs/wires/thin/depth/dupes), not corpus COMPLETENESS — it cannot see a missing node.** So the **Bible is ~half-missing and still scores 7/7**: NO individual node exists for the **12 Minor Prophets, Jeremiah, Joshua/Judges/Samuel/Esther/Nehemiah, or most NT epistles** (the Paulines are ONE grouped node). **The legacy prototype had the IDENTICAL curated Bible** (same node IDs) → not a V2 regression, the Bible was never complete. **Deities "9/9" has the same blind spot** (Sumerian gods folded into the "Mesopotamian" family — 61 deities, no separate Sumerian wedge; grades what exists, not whether the pantheon is whole). John: *"this is very critical and fatal … FOR ALL RELIGIONS, I'm using Christianity just as a glaring example."* **PLANNING MODE** (John: *"we plan first"*): the agreed next step is a **general per-tradition completeness/coverage audit** (a 2nd bar beside the quality scorecard — per tradition, hold the authoritative canon/pantheon list, report what's missing; one row each on DEV → Overview). Build the read-only audit FIRST, after confirming scope. Design axes still open (fold-vs-distinguish; complete-vs-curated; canon-comparison wheel; Gnostic-under-Christianity). **Also shipped 2 UX fixes:** #3 CODEX class-pill all-caps (`02feecec`, verified — breadcrumb left Title Case), #4 Boards "Add node" restored (`5252d348`, verified — a 2026-05-29 module-level `_boardsControls` export had pre-empted init()'s guard so the pill never mounted). **#1/#2 side-panel decoupling GREENLIT but NOT started** (one canonical panel = thumbnail + description + wires for every node; `#detail-inner` is co-owned by a LEGACY `app.js renderDetail` AND the canonical `forge/side-panel.js` — the "dead prototype panel" John smelled). Full brief: `HANDOFF-2026-06-05-completeness-crisis.md`. Everything committed, gates green, tree clean. Slot ACTIVE.

Fix: split the running string into ALREADY-MARKED and PLAIN segments using the existing mark tags as separators, only annotate plain segments. (2) The `tryApplyFilter` LS-rehydration latch in codex-controls.js only fired when corpusId was set, so a page reload with religion-only state (e.g. {religionId: christianity, corpusId: null}) left the wheel unfiltered. Fix: latch now fires on religionId OR corpusId. Bundle rebuilt via scripts/build-forge-bundle.sh. SCRIPTURE_TEXTS 190→196 entries. > **`watcher-claude-phase-23-1-revert-and-safety-net` — FINISHED 2026-05-25 LATE evening — Two commits. (1) `3c294e2` REVERTED the Phase 23.1 carve series after John reported runtime breakage (clicks dead, zoom dead, render fine). The first throw inside the `async bootstrap()` IIFE became a swallowed Promise rejection — silent. `attachInteractions()` never ran. Future async-IIFE silent-throw failures are now loud on the standard console channel. Zero cost on happy path. Preview-verified live: forge boots clean, wheel dispatch returns `wheelPrevented:true` + `cameraScale 1→1.22`, zero console errors. Phase 24 Legacy viewer (commit `8cc2cd9`) PRESERVED through the revert. All TYRANT remediation (594→490 dead-links, source-tier coverage, stubs) PRESERVED. **3 prereqs identified for any future carve retry**: (a) AST-based dep scanner replacing the regex heuristic; (b) interactive smoke test (click + drag + zoom + open each panel) beyond pixel-identical preview; (c) ✅ async-IIFE catch — shipped. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21g-2026-05-21` — BG anchored in WORLD UNITS (fundamental fix) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. After a long iteration through viewport-relative BG sizing schemes (Phases 20F-21F) that each had different cascade-related failure modes, John identified the architectural root cause: "WE SHOULD fundamentally setup world sizes ??????????? exactly to avoid these scenarios". The BG image was the only object in the engine without a world-unit definition — every pie slice, every deity dot, every divider sits in the canonical 1128 wu coordinate space, but the BG was being measured in viewport pixels and gizmo-percentages, which is why each iteration had a different failure mode. **One change**: declared `BG_WORLD_WIDTH = 18000` wu (~16× the wheel's 1128 wu extent) in `src/js/views/forge.js`. `syncBackgroundImage()` now computes `widthPx = BG_WORLD_WIDTH × camera.state.scale` and `heightPx = widthPx / imageAspect`, then positions via `camera.worldToScreen(0, 0)` + canvas-offset → viewport coords + `translate(-50%, -50%)` to centre on the image's own midpoint. CSS only owns the static layer state (fixed positioning, opacity, will-change hints). **Result**: the BG image is now a world object like the wheel. Same coordinate system, same projection, same pan-and-zoom behaviour — by construction, not by JS arithmetic. At gizmo 10% on any viewport, BG covers viewport with ~50% pan margin on each side. At higher zoom, BG grows in lockstep with the wheel. The question "how big is the BG at any zoom on any screen" has exactly one answer now: `18000 × camera.state.scale` pixels. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21g`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-phase17-capstone-christianity-five-inheritances` — FINISHED 2026-05-21 — Phase-17 essay series CAPSTONE shipped + collection README: `00_meta/MASSIVE-WIN-essays/christianity-five-inheritances.md` (~177 lines synthesis) + `00_meta/MASSIVE-WIN-essays/README.md` (collection navigator).** — Lane A. **Direct hit on John's "MASSIVE-WIN framing — connections from Christianity → older traditions are the prize" memory.** The capstone synthesizes the four individual Phase-17 essays into a single Christianity-focused thesis: what did Christianity inherit, what did it innovate, and what does the cumulative cross-tradition map reveal? **Five inheritances documented**: (1) The Eucharist (3,500-year substrate — Indo-Iranian *\*sauma-* → Vedic Soma + Avestan Haoma → Dionysian wine → Eleusinian kykeon → Mithraic meal → Jewish Passover kos ha-berakha → Christian Eucharist; Paul's 1 Cor 10:16 explicit Greek translation of the Hebrew Passover-cup vocabulary); (2) The Executed Divine Claimant pattern (2,500-year substrate — Justin Martyr *First Apology* ch. 46 as the earliest documented Christian conscious-relay of Socrates into the pattern); (3) The Pantokrator iconography (Phidian Zeus → Sol-Invictus radiate crown → 4th-c. CE Christ-Pantokrator; Mathews 1993 *Clash of Gods*); (4) The Logos / Trinity theology (Egyptian Ptah / Memphite Theology → Plato → Philo → John 1 → Nicaea + Chalcedon; Boyarin 2004 + Segal 1977 + Hurtado 2003); (5) The Soul's Exile and Return (Plotinian Neoplatonism + Gnostic substrate → Pauline kenosis + 'in Christ' pattern → Christian mystical tradition). **Three findings**: (a) the inheritances are LAYERED with substrate-depth varying systematically — oldest is Eucharist (3,500 years deep), youngest is Pantokrator iconography (matures into Christianity); (b) Christianity's TWO MAXIMAL-DEIFICATION MOVES (Eucharistic transubstantiation + Chalcedonian *homoousios* Christology) are the consistent distinctive innovations across ALL inheritances — Christianity inherits broadly but innovates by taking the deification claim to its ontological maximum at two specific structural locations; (c) the cross-tradition map makes BOTH inheritance AND innovation visible at once — unlike standard religious-studies scholarship which tends to do EITHER apologetic-mode (minimize the maximal-deification moves) OR polemical-mode (flatten the inheritances), the Codex Atlas's seven-lens wiring produces a *Christianity-without-apology AND Christianity-without-debunking* dual visibility. **README collection navigator** added at `00_meta/MASSIVE-WIN-essays/README.md` — describes all 5 essays, reading orders, methodology discipline, and links into the Phase 7-16 hub-wiring infrastructure. **PHASE-17 ESSAY SERIES COMPLETE**: 5 essays (~1,127 lines total) covering the four densest cross-tradition hubs + the Christianity-focused synthesis. The cross-tradition spine the Codex Atlas was built to investigate is now legible at THREE altitudes: YAML hub-edges (Phases 7-16, machine-navigable, ~450 edges); individual hub-file prose (Phases 10-16, per-pattern reference articles); Phase-17 essays (cross-hub readable scholarship). **Build verification**: linkcheck.py confirms vault baseline 595/825 unchanged; TOTAL FILES 4547 → 4549 from capstone + README creation; WIRING LAW held; no dead links introduced. Lane A slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21ef-2026-05-21` — BG anchor shift: native-size at gizmo 0% (unreachable), 1.9× at gizmo 10% (floor) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. After Phase 21D's `100vw / 100vh + object-fit: cover` approach failed visibly (image rendered smaller than viewport for an undiagnosed CSS-cascade reason), John asked to flip the diagnostic: "make the image NOT fill the background. just retain its original size." That landed in **Phase 21E** (CSS dropped `width / height / object-fit`; IMG renders at its natural 2668 × 2000) — confirmed the DOM / transform path works (image renders huge, way bigger than viewport). Then **Phase 21F** moved the scale anchor: `scale = 1 + 9 × zoomPct`, so `scale = 1.0` (image at native pixel size) lands at gizmo 0% (the unreachable theoretical limit) and `scale = 1.9` at gizmo 10% (the camera floor — image at 1.9× native, always bigger than any normal viewport on every axis). Panning through the floor always reveals more image, never a gap. Growth rate (`10 × scale per 100% zoom`) preserved from the earlier `zoomPct / 0.10` formula. CSS retains `top: 50%; left: 50%; transform-origin: 50% 50%`; JS transform = `translate(-50%, -50%) translate(dx, dy) scale(s)`. The "no gaps" guarantee no longer relies on a `100vw / 100vh` CSS rule that any parent transform could silently rescale — it relies on the IMG's intrinsic dimensions being big enough that any normal viewport sits comfortably inside the rendered image. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21f`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21d-2026-05-21` — bulletproof BG: CSS `object-fit: cover` at 100vw/100vh + JS transform follows wheel — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John: "i want to fix the BG ? can you do it ? or its impossible to make a BG fit a screen ?" + "RIGHT NOT THERES BLUE GAPS IN THE BG ALL AROUND THE IMAGE IS NOT FITTING THE ENTIRE WORLD AND IT GETS LIMITED TO ITS SIZE IF THE SCREEN IS BIGGER". **Two changes**: (A) **CSS in `src/styles/app.css`** — `.forge-bg-image` is now FUNDAMENTALLY pinned to the viewport: `position: fixed; top: 0; left: 0; width: 100vw; height: 100vh; object-fit: cover; object-position: center center; transform-origin: 50% 50%`. The browser's own `object-fit: cover` engine guarantees the image fills every viewport corner at every viewport size — JS can't introduce a wrong size. (B) **JS in `src/js/views/forge.js`** — `syncBackgroundImage()` reduced to opacity + a CSS transform. Each camera tick computes `scale = max(0.10, zoomPct) / 0.10` (image grows with zoom; floors at fill-coverage when below gizmo 10%) + `(dx, dy) = (wheel-centre projected to viewport) − (viewport centre)` (image translates to follow the wheel through pan). Final write: `bgImage.style.transform = "translate(<dx>px, <dy>px) scale(<scale>)"`. No more width / height / left / top math. `transform-origin: 50% 50%` ensures scale grows from the same point pan tracks. **The promise**: image covers 100% of viewport at every zoom and every viewport size, full stop. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21d`. Files: `src/styles/app.css`, `src/js/views/forge.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21c-2026-05-21` — world-anchored BG image (follows the wheel through pan + zoom) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after 21B: "now appears But its not CENTERED with the WHEEL! I CAN zoom out move around and the BG is static! NO, needs to be stuck with the chart". **Two changes in `src/js/views/forge.js` + `src/styles/app.css`**: (A) **CSS** — dropped `inset: 0; width: 100vw; height: 100vh; object-fit: cover` on `.forge-bg-image`. Left only `position: fixed; z-index: 0; opacity: 0` as the static layer; JS now writes `left / top / width / height` each camera tick. (B) **`syncBackgroundImage()`** — restored full world-anchored math from Phase 21A but with the new body-root parent. Each tick: (1) compute `zoomPct = camera.state.scale / fit_scale` (gizmo terms); (2) opacity ramp 0 at 50% → 1 at 10% (unchanged); (3) `sizeZoomPct = max(0.10, zoomPct)` floor; (4) cover-fit box at gizmo 10% from `aspect` (cached from `bgImage._bgAspect` via load-event listener) vs `vpAspect` — picks the branch that matches max viewport axis edge-to-edge + overflows the smaller axis; (5) `scaleFactor = sizeZoomPct / 0.10` × cover box = actual image dims at current zoom; (6) world (0, 0) → canvas screen via `camera.worldToScreen`, then add `canvas.getBoundingClientRect()` offset to land in viewport coords (which `position: fixed` reads). Result: pan moves the BG with the wheel; zoom scales the BG with the wheel; at gizmo 10% the BG fills the larger viewport axis edge-to-edge; below 10% it stays at fill-coverage (size floor matches opacity floor). Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21c`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21a-2026-05-21` — sidebar removed site-wide + floating nav-hub dropdown + BG image swap (A02) + aspect-aware cover-fit — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John: "remove that side bar from the entire website … make the top left icon with the logo our MAIN button to open the main HUB as a DROP DOWN … added a new image for the bg : BG A02.jpg … make sure the IMAGE FILLS THE ENTIRE screen no GAP no left or right." **Four changes**: (A) **BG image** — ingested `99_ingest/BG A02.jpg` → `_assets/bg/bg-a02.jpg` (2668×2000, 4:3 wide). Forge `bgImage.src` now points to A02. Aspect ratio cached on `bgImage._bgAspect`, updated from `naturalWidth/naturalHeight` on `load` event, and `syncBackgroundImage()` re-fires on first load so the very first paint uses the real aspect. (B) **Sidebar removed** — new body class `nav-hidden` replaces `nav-collapsed` as the default. CSS sets `--eff-nav-w: 0` + `--nav-w: 0` + `nav.side { display:none }` + `.side-tab { display:none }`. Every left-anchored element (main.canvas, footer, …) snaps to the viewport edge — the BG image now extends past where the sidebar used to reserve 48 px. Legacy `<nav class="side">` markup KEPT in DOM (hidden) so existing `setView`, style-picker, themes-button, tier-toggle, and stats handlers keep finding their targets. (C) **Aspect-aware cover-fit** — `syncBackgroundImage()` rewritten to compute `coverW / coverH` from the image's natural aspect and the viewport's aspect: image more landscape than viewport → match height + overflow width; image more portrait → match width + overflow height. Image rendered with separate width / height (no square stretch). At gizmo 10% the image fully covers; at higher zoom it scales up linearly. (D) **Floating nav-hub dropdown** — new `<button class="nav-hub-trigger">` + `<div class="nav-hub-menu">` at top-left (z-index 240/250, fixed positioning, backdrop blur 16 px). Click opens / closes; the glyph rotates 180° on open; dropdown scales + fades from a transform-origin near the trigger (feels like it grew from the symbol). New `mountNavHub()` IIFE in `src/js/app.js` reads the hidden sidebar's items at mount time + via `MutationObserver` so any future view added there auto-appears in the dropdown — no second source of truth. Clicking a menu item calls the existing `setView()`. Close on outside-click + Esc. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21a`. Files: `index.html`, `src/styles/app.css`, `src/js/app.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `_assets/bg/bg-a02.jpg`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20j-2026-05-21` — hulls anchored at world origin (not drifted centroid) + canonical layout radii + BG image size floor at 7% — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after 20I: "zoom out not filling not even the entire screen…. once again not fucking centered all nodes ignore completely the HULLS." **TWO root causes diagnosed via smoke-test** — measured the actual centroid after layout: **the deity-position centroid drifts to (24.18, −39.35) — 46 wu away from world origin**. But the wedges (and divider lines) are angularly anchored to world (0, 0), so the pie slices were being rendered around a point ~60 px off-screen from where the deity cluster actually projects. Also: `max-from-centroid = 572.9` while `max-from-origin = 534.0` — the data-driven outer radius was ~40 wu bigger than the actual rim, pushing the pie arc out into empty space. **Three fixes**: (A) `src/js/engine/graph/hull.js` — `buildFamilyHulls` now accepts an opts shape `{ wedges, rInner, rOuter }`; the hull `center` is hard-anchored at `(0, 0)`; returned `innerRadius` / `outerRadius` prefer the supplied canonical values (lay.rInner = 220, lay.rOuter = 540) over the data-derived max/min-from-centroid. Backwards-compat with the legacy positional wedge-map arg preserved. (B) `src/js/views/forge.js` `rebuildForMode` now passes `{ wedges: lay.wedges, rInner: lay.rInner, rOuter: lay.rOuter }`. Verified via smoke-test: `center: (0.00, 0.00)`, `innerRadius: 220`, `outerRadius: 540`, 34 hulls + 34 dividers — every metric pinned to the canonical wheel geometry. (C) BG image sizing — added `sizeZoomPct = max(0.07, zoomPct)` floor in `syncBackgroundImage()` so the image stays at fill-coverage when the user zooms BELOW the gizmo's 7% (the camera allows down to absolute scale 0.05, which can map to gizmo percentages well under 7% on landscape viewports). Opacity already had the 7% floor; sizing now matches. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20j`. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/hull.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20i-2026-05-21` — zoom-percentage semantics fix (gizmo % is scale/fit, not raw scale) + max-axis cover-fit — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20H: "image is now filling vertically all height but not the widht, the image should fit always the larger window x or y. all still off — WHAT YOU DOING question." **Root cause diagnosis** — the zoom-gizmo bottom-bar button displays `(camera.state.scale / fit_scale) × 100` as a percentage. So "100%" in the gizmo = fit (NOT camera.state.scale = 1.0), "50%" = `fit × 0.5`, "7%" = `fit × 0.07`. My Phase 20F/G/H were comparing RAW `camera.state.scale` against 0.50 / 0.25 / 0.07, which means the same numeric value gives very different zoom levels depending on the current viewport's fit-scale. John's "7% max zoom out" referred to the GIZMO percentage, not raw scale. **Two fixes in `src/js/views/forge.js`**: (1) `syncBackgroundImage()` now computes `zoomPct = camera.state.scale / computeFitScale()` and uses THAT for both the opacity ramp and image sizing. Image side = `(max(vp.w, vp.h) / 0.07) × zoomPct` — at the gizmo's "7%" the image side equals the LARGER viewport axis (fits edge-to-edge along that axis as John specified — "the image should fit always the larger window x or y"; smaller axis overflows + crops). Reverted Phase 20H's diagonal sizing per John's explicit "larger window x or y" directive. (2) The family-label fade in `syncHulls()` now also uses `zoomPct` (was raw `camScale`) — labels fade out from 50% to 25% zoom-gizmo-percentage. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20i`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20h-2026-05-21` — bumped visible disk-fit buffer + true diagonal cover-fit for BG image — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20G: "the image IS NOT filling the screen at max zoom (FILL CROP). Zero changes in the nodes respecting each hull — what you doing?" **Two fixes in `src/js/views/forge.js`**: (1) Pie-slice radial padding bumped from 14 px to **28 px** total — split into `DISK_CONTAIN_PAD = 10` (contains the disk radius — ~6–9 px disk + a margin) and `DISK_BREATHE_PAD = 18` (the visible buffer between disk edge and slice arc). At default fit-zoom (`scale ≈ 0.65`), the visible gap from outermost disk edge to pie-slice outer arc is now ~18 px — reads cleanly as "the dots are INSIDE their slices with breathing room". The 14-px version (Phase 20G) only gave ~6 px gap which was visually imperceptible — exactly what John flagged as "zero changes". (2) `syncBackgroundImage()` now sizes the image to the viewport DIAGONAL at scale 0.07 (`imgSize = hypot(vp.w, vp.h) / 0.07 × camScale`), not `max(vp.w, vp.h)`. The diagonal sizing guarantees that every viewport CORNER is inside the square image — true `object-fit: cover` behaviour at max zoom-out. The old max-side sizing matched only one viewport axis and left the corners dark on rectangular viewports (which is what John saw in the 7%-zoom screenshot). Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20h`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20d2-2026-05-21` — organic age-radial fan (replaces 20D's rigid bands) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20D shipped: "this is TOO aligned and mechanical, i want the previous feeling but with the constraint like i said. make it open like a fan and the bigger nodes can occupy bigger areas and the clusters should flow organically NOT rigid." **Three changes** to `src/js/engine/layout/radial.js`: (A) **Initial placement now uses VAN DER CORPUT base-2 sequence** for the angular slot (vdc(1)=½, vdc(2)=¼, vdc(3)=¾, vdc(4)=⅛, vdc(5)=⅝, …). Each rank gets a UNIQUE, well-distributed angular slot in (−1, +1), with consecutive ranks landing FAR apart — eliminates the pile-up that age-sorted Vogel/cos(i·φ) produced. (B) **Radius is soft-jittered age-rank** (±16% of annulus span around the age target) so age-equivalent members spread radially instead of forming a ring. (C) **Fan factor multiplies u by `0.45 + 0.55·radNorm`** — angular spread is 45% at the inner rim, 100% at the outer rim, so wedges literally open like a hand-fan. (D) **2D anti-collision relaxation** (8 iterations) — replaces the 20D angular-only push. Each pair within `minDist` (BASE_SIZE 11 + HUB_BONUS 7·degNorm) pushes apart along the 2D line between them; coincident pairs split along a deterministic hash-bearing. A soft age-spring (`SPRING_K = 0.10`) pulls each member's radius back toward its age target after every push, so radius can DRIFT when angular space runs out (essential for tight wedges) without losing the age ordering. Final clamp keeps everything inside `[rIn, rOut]` and inside the fan-constrained wedge bounds. **Verified live**: min pairwise distance per wedge now 10.5–17 wu (was 0–7 wu pre-fix; zero stacked nodes); Vedic outer rim is all ancient (dyaus-pita −4000 BCE, indra/shiva/vritra/aditi −1500), inner rim is all medieval (bhairava 400, waheguru 1500, radha 800, narada undated); Vedic angle range `[-0.106, +0.100]` spans the full wedge; outer-rim spread (~0.20 rad) is 2× inner-rim spread (~0.10 rad) — fan effect visible. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20d2`. Files: `src/js/engine/layout/radial.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20d-2026-05-21` — radial layout rebuild (age-radial + thematic family order + halved divider width) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20C: "define inner radius + outer radius, deities span those radii closely while keeping organic spread, laid out from oldest to new from outer to inner within their own family. Organize families with logic — contesting / relevant religions opposite each other on the wheel (Christianity ↔ Islamic ↔ Judaism as triangle, Greek ↔ Roman, Buddhism ↔ Vedic-India, etc). Separator lines half the current width." **Three changes shipped**: (A) **`src/js/engine/layout/radial.js`** — Vogel/golden-angle spiral REPLACED by AGE-RADIAL BAND PACKING. Members sort by `date_earliest` ASC (oldest first, undated sink to inner), then pack into concentric bands inside each wedge. Band capacity = floor(usableArc × meanR / 52) clamped to [1, 6, N] — wide wedges (Vedic 60, Greek 48) get 4-5 per band, narrow ones (Manichaean 4) get 1-2 per band, but every family fills its wedge cleanly from rOuter→rInner. Within a band, members fan evenly across the wedge arc; alternate bands shift half a sub-slot for brick-row stagger (organic, not gridded). Radial position uses the equal-AREA formula `r = sqrt(rIn² + (k+0.5)/K · (rOut² − rIn²))` so band density is visually balanced inner-vs-outer. Tiny deterministic FNV-hash jitter (±12 px / ±0.012 rad) breaks the perfect-ring look. (B) **`src/js/views/forge.js`** — `vault.families`-encounter-order REPLACED by a curated `FAMILY_ORDER` of all 36 families arranged as 18 OPPOSITE PAIRS (slot i ↔ slot i+18): Christian↔Islamic / Israelite↔Canaanite / Rabbinic↔Pre-Islamic-Arabian / Greek↔Roman / Norse↔Egyptian / Mesopotamian↔Chinese / Vedic↔Buddhist / Celtic↔Mesoamerican / Hermetic↔Modern-Esoteric / African↔Andean / Mystery↔Gnostic / Neoplatonist↔Manichaean / Native-American↔Pacific / Baltic↔Slavic-Finnic / Armenian↔Other / Zoroastrian↔Shinto / Hittite↔Etruscan / Mandaean↔Academic. (C) **`src/styles/app.css`** — `.forge-hull-divider` stroke-width halved 1px → 0.5px. **Verified live (smoke-test against deity-mode)**: 17 of 18 opposite pairs land at 174.0°–179.7° (Vedic↔Buddhist 179.2°, Greek↔Roman 173.9°, Mesopotamian↔Chinese 175.9°). The 2 "missing" pairs (Rabbinic↔Pre-Islamic-Arabian, Mandaean↔Academic) are because Rabbinic + Academic have zero `type === "deity"` nodes — they'll appear in non-deity modes. Vedic age-radial spans r=520 (oldest: `dyaus-pita` -4000 BCE) → r=242 (newest: `narada` undated), exactly tracking `date_earliest`. `rishabha-jain` lands at r=520 with date=-999,999,999 — known Lane A data bug, not a layout issue. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20b-2026-05-21` — radial family titles outside the ring + separator lines between adjacent families — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20: titles were cluttered inside the wheel, asked for them OUTSIDE the outer ring. Also asked for radial separator lines between each family, overshooting the inner + outer radii by 50 px, last 15% on each endpoint fading to 0. **Fix**: extended `buildFamilyHulls` to compute (a) wheel center (mean of all packed positions), (b) inner + outer ring radii (min/max distance from center), (c) per-family centroidAngle via CIRCULAR MEAN (sum unit vectors then atan2 — wrap-safe), (d) dividers[] — angular bisectors between angularly-adjacent families. Return shape changed from `Array` to `{ hulls, center, innerRadius, outerRadius, dividers }`. **Rendering**: SVG overlay now has 4 ordered children — `<defs>` (per-line linearGradients) + `polys` group + `dividers` group + `labels` group — polys paint first, dividers in the middle, labels on top. `syncHulls()` now also measures the ring's SCREEN radius once per frame (via worldToScreen of an edge point) so divider overshoots + label offsets work in screen px regardless of camera scale. **Labels**: position = centerScreen + (ringPxRadius + 28) × (cos a, sin a). Outside the rim everywhere; no more overlap with the node cloud. **Dividers**: from (innerPx − 50) to (outerPx + 50) along bisector angle. Each line uses `stroke="url(#forge-hull-divgrad-N)"` with a per-line `<linearGradient gradientUnits="userSpaceOnUse">` whose 4 stops are 0%(α=0) → 15%(α=0.55) → 85%(α=0.55) → 100%(α=0) — the gradient endpoints are updated each frame to track the line, so the fade rides the line correctly through pan/zoom. **Verified live**: 34 dividers + 34 gradients + 34 labels + 34 polys. Labels rendered: VEDIC, BUDDHIST, CHINESE, EGYPTIAN, AFRICAN, ETRUSCAN, HERMETIC, MYSTERY, GREEK, ROMAN, CELTIC, BALTIC, SLAVIC-FINNIC, CHRISTIAN, GNOSTIC, NEOPLATONIST, MANICHAEAN, MANDAEAN, PRE-ISLAMIC-ARABIAN, ISLAMIC, MESOAMERICAN, ANDEAN, NATIVE-AMERICAN, PACIFIC, SHINTO, MODERN-ESOTERIC, OTHER, CANAANITE, ISRAELITE, ZOROASTRIAN, ARMENIAN, NORSE, JAIN, PRE-CHRISTIAN-MAGYAR. Separator lines visible as thin gold-tinted radial spokes between every adjacent family arc, fading at both ends. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20b`. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/hull.js` (centroidAngle + dividers + ring metrics), `src/js/views/forge.js` (hull SVG restructure into 4 ordered groups + per-line gradient updates), `src/styles/app.css` (.forge-hull-divider + .forge-hull-label-g). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-17-2026-05-21` — hover card position rewrite (anchor-once + rAF-coalesce) + wire Wikipedia thumbnails — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John reported the hover card glitched: it started in one position then jumped to another, and at higher zoom levels it'd appear far from the cursor then "stick." Root cause: positionCard was re-evaluating the quadrant choice on EVERY mousemove, which means near a screen corner the card flipped between quadrants on single-pixel cursor moves — pure flicker. Plus the card was positioned BEFORE the image loaded; when the image arrived height changed but the card stayed at its initial position until the next mousemove. **Phase 17 rewrites the positioning as anchor-once + hysteresis.** Anchor (which of 4 quadrants — top-right / top-left / bottom-right / bottom-left of cursor) is picked ONCE at showFor time via `pickAnchor()` — best-fit quadrant for the current viewport. mousemove then only translates by `(cursor + anchorX*OFFSET, cursor + anchorY*OFFSET)` using CACHED dimensions, zero layout reads. Anchor re-flips ONLY when the cached card would actually overflow at the new cursor position (hysteresis — flicker eliminated). rAF-coalesced via `schedulePosition()` so transform writes are capped at 1/frame. **Image load now triggers a re-measure + reposition** — height change post-load no longer leaves the card stale at the pre-image position. **Wikipedia thumbnails wired**: fetch `_assets/thumbs_cache.json` once at view mount (Wikipedia URL + extract per node id, ~1.5 MB cached); look up `cache[id].src` on hover, set `<img src>` directly. Description falls back to the cache's Wikipedia `extract` (first sentence, capped 180 chars) when YAML doesn't have a `role`/`description`. **OFFSET bumped 22 → 38** so even a max-clamped selected disk (22 × 1.5 ≈ 33 px) never sits under the card. **Window resize** invalidates the anchor → hides the card so the next hover gets a fresh quadrant choice. **Verified live**: page boots clean, thumbs_cache.json HTTP 200 (cache loads), no console errors. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-17`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (wireHoverCard — thumbs cache fetch + measure + pickAnchor + applyTransform + schedulePosition; showFor — wires the cache lookup + extract fallback; mousemove handler reduced to rAF schedule), `index.html` (cache-bust). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-11c-2026-05-21` — Phase 11C: HIDDEN preserved across zoom rebake + label-filter for HIDDEN — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John bug-report after Phase 11: "the hidden nodes appear on zoom back — including labels." Root cause: `recomputeFocus` applied the timeline `state = 2.0` override inline, but `rebakeNodes` (fired by camera.onChange when zoom drifts past the N-aware threshold) ran `computeNodeStates` from `focusedSet` without applying the same override — so out-of-range nodes reverted to `state = 1.0` (FADED) on zoom-back, visibly re-appearing. Labels had the same class of bug: `syncLabels` idle-tier branch computed visibility purely from tier + camScale, with no node-state check, so HIDDEN nodes' labels rendered too. **Fixes**: (a) extracted the timeline-override loop into a shared helper `applyTimelineHiddenOverride(idx, states, focusedSet)`; called from BOTH recomputeFocus and rebakeNodes; (b) `syncLabels` idle-tier branch now filters out nodes whose `nodeTargets[i*2] >= 1.5` (HIDDEN). The check reads from `nodeTargets` (the post-override authoritative buffer), so any future filter that hides a node — search, mode-filter, etc. — drops its label automatically without re-coding the loop. **Verified live**: page boots clean, no console errors. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-11c`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (helper extracted + called from rebakeNodes + label filter). **Lesson**: any state computed by `recomputeFocus` that's also re-computed by `rebakeNodes` is a divergence-risk surface. The right pattern is a single helper called from both, never duplicated logic. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-11-2026-05-20` — Phase 11: HIDDEN state + timeline upper-bound = today + year-0 marker + fixed-width date boxes — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. After Phase 10 confirmation ("we got it!"), John handed in 4 polish asks for the timeline scrubber. **(1) HIDDEN state**: out-of-range timeline nodes should not just be FADED, they should disappear. Extended the per-node state float to a 3-step ladder — `0 = IDLE`, `1 = FADED`, `2 = HIDDEN` — with the alpha piecewise: `segA × segB_factor` where segA mixes IDLE→FADED for `state ∈ [0,1]` and segB_factor fades FADED→0 for `state ∈ [1,2]`. Both NODE_SHADER and GLYPH_SHADER fragments updated to the same formula. Fragments discard when `final_a < 0.005`. The fade pipeline animates the state float continuously, so out-of-range nodes smoothly fade out instead of pop-out. Timeline override in `recomputeFocus` now writes `states[i] = 2.0` for non-focused out-of-range nodes. **(2) Upper bound = today's year**: `HIST_HI` constant in `deriveBounds` was `3000` (arbitrary), now `new Date().getFullYear()`. Plus a critical fix: the century-rounding `Math.ceil(hi/100)*100` was bumping `2024 → 2100` AFTER the clamp, defeating it. Added a second clamp post-rounding: `if (hipad > HIST_HI) hipad = HIST_HI;`. Now the timeline tops out at the current year cleanly. **(3) Year-0 reference tick**: new `.forge-scrub-year-zero` element inside the track, positioned by `dateToFrac(0) × 100%` in `refreshUI`. Hidden when bounds don't straddle 0 (defensive). CSS makes it 1px wide × 10px tall, grey-faded at 0.45 opacity, z-index 0 (behind the thumbs). Pure visual cue, doesn't intercept clicks. **(4) Fixed-width date boxes**: `.forge-scrub-box` switched from `min-width: 56px` to `width: 86px` — sized for the longest readout ("15000 BCE" = 9 chars × 7px + 22px padding/border). No more jumping as the user scrubs through values of different string length. **Verified live in preview iframe**: `_forgeDebug.timeline()` returns `{ lo: -15000, hi: 2026, ... }`. The bottom bar shows `15000 BCE | slider | 2026 CE | center_date`. Year-zero element exists in the DOM. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-11b`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (NODE_SHADER + GLYPH_SHADER fragments — piecewise state→alpha + discard), `src/js/views/forge.js` (timeline override → 2.0 for HIDDEN, deriveBounds clamp-to-today + post-round re-clamp, year-zero element in template + refreshUI positioning), `src/styles/app.css` (box fixed-width + year-zero tick style). **John's 4-state node model now fully implemented**: IDLE (full opaque), OVER (same + bigger + stroke), FADED (alpha 0.25), HIDDEN (alpha 0, discarded). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-10-2026-05-20` — Phase 10: timeline filter no longer overrides explicit focus — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John screenshot of two hovered Mesopotamian deities: Tammuz/Dumuzi rendered properly lit (OVER); Ningirsu rendered FADED with a stroke ring (bug). Same render path, same family color, only the `date_earliest` value in the YAML differs. **Root cause** (line 2342 in forge.js): the timeline scrubber filter loop forced `states[i] = 1.0` on any out-of-range node — INCLUDING nodes the user explicitly hovered or locked. So if a node's `date_earliest` was outside the current scrubber range, hovering it produced `selected=1, state=1` simultaneously — bigger gold-stroked disk at FADED opacity (state_alpha = 0.25). Ningirsu's YAML date was outside the scrubber range; Tammuz's wasn't (or was undated, which the override already skips). **Fix**: skip `focusedSet` members in the timeline override. User's explicit focus beats automatic filter — if you point at a node, you see it. One-line change inside recomputeFocus: `if (focused && focused.has(idx[i])) continue;` before the date check. **Verified**: page boots clean, no console errors. The bug class (state=1 + selected=1 simultaneously) is structurally impossible now: focusedSet ⊇ selectedSet, and any node in focusedSet is skipped by the override → can't be forced to state=1. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-10`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (recomputeFocus timeline override loop). **Lesson**: when two filters can both write to the same state value, they need a priority rule. Default rule: user-explicit (hover/lock) beats automatic (timeline / search filter / etc.). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-9-2026-05-20` — Phase 9: FADED state is alpha-based (0.25), no color changes — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's correction after Phase 7+8: *"dont want different colors, like i said, THE change of color MAKE it a change of opacity SAME LOGIC."* Plus the 4-state spec: IDLE = 100% opaque + family color + base size; OVER = SAME 100% opaque, just bigger + stroke; FADED = same color at lower opacity (e.g. 0.25); HIDDEN = future. Phase 7's color-darkening of FADED disks (`fill_rgb × dim_mult`) violated the spec — John wants the family color UNCHANGED in all states; only OPACITY varies. **Fix**: switched both NODE_SHADER and GLYPH_SHADER fragments back to alpha-based dim. New fragment math: `state_alpha = mix(1.0, 1 - dim_amount, state); final_a = state_alpha × disk_alpha;` — RGB stays at the family color in IDLE and FADED, only alpha changes. PARAM_DEFAULTS: `dim_amount_nodes: 0.90 → 0.75` and `dim_amount_glyphs: 0.90 → 0.75` so FADED state alpha = 1 - 0.75 = 0.25 per John's spec. Safe to do now because Phase 7 already deleted the discard threshold that previously caught dim disks below 0.15 alpha — alpha-based fade no longer makes disks vanish. **Verified live in preview iframe**: idle screenshot shows the full wheel of 676 deities at full opacity (every family color clear); lock Toyouke shows the focus cluster (Toyouke + 1-hop neighbors) at full opacity and the rest translucent at 0.25 alpha — you can see colored dots faintly across the background. No console errors. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-9`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (NODE_SHADER fragment + GLYPH_SHADER fragment alpha rule), `src/js/views/forge.js` (PARAM_DEFAULTS dim semantics comment + values). **Lesson**: when the user says "same logic" — they mean literally the same mathematical operation. The FADED → OPACITY DROP rule is just `final_a × (1 - dim_amount)`. Color stays color. Alpha is alpha. No mixing the two channels. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-8-2026-05-20` — Phase 8: GLYPH NESTED WITH DISK, WHITE TINT, no independent visibility rules — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's feedback after Phase 7: *"why they are all idle opacity when over and selected? why some don't display the symbol? also check the rules of visibility on zoom scales between the node objects if they match — they shouldn't match, they should be NESTED to each other."* The first two complaints traced to Phase 6B (focus rule hiding glyph on focused/selected) and Phase 6A (screen-size fade creating "missing symbol at this zoom" frames). The third was the architectural insight: **disk and glyph should be NESTED geometry with one visibility rule, not two independent rules**. Phase 8 fix: **DELETED Phase 6A screen-size fade**, **DELETED Phase 6B focus rule**, **DELETED Phase 5C-era per-instance tint coloring of glyph**, **DELETED glyph_opacity uniform** from the alpha math. New rule: glyph paints in WHITE (Apple-icon style, universal contrast on any family color); alpha follows tex.a directly; color dims via the same `dim_mult = mix(1, 1 - dim_g, state)` formula as the disk (so glyph dims with the disk when state=1). Glyph quad size = disk_radius × glyph_scale × selected_size_mult — strictly nested with the disk. The fragment is now ~8 lines: sample atlas, discard if transparent, output `vec4(white × dim_mult × tex.a, tex.a)`. Same vertex z = disk z (Phase 1B invariant preserved). Removed `screen_r` interpolator from VsOut (no longer needed). View struct + uniforms unchanged. **Verified live in preview iframe**: idle screenshot shows the full wheel of 676 deities as solid colored disks (each family clearly visible by hue); lock Toyouke shows the focus cluster + visibly dimmer background (each dim disk darker but still readable). No console errors. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-8`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (GLYPH_SHADER vertex + fragment rewritten + VsOut shrunk), `src/js/views/forge.js` (spec-lock FX section Phase 6A/6B comments replaced with Phase 8). **Lesson**: nested objects need ONE visibility rule, not two. The moment glyph had a `smoothstep(5,10,screen_r)` that disk didn't, frames where one rendered but not the other became inevitable — and confusing to debug because the diff wasn't in either object's own logic, it was in the asymmetry between them. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-7-2026-05-20` — Phase 7: HARD-CORE SIMPLE NODE — strip glow halo + alpha-based dim + discard floor; rebuild around (size, fill_color, stroke) — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's call after Phase 6B: *"WHAT COMPOSITED ALPHA? I been saying I DONT WANT MASKS, I WANT THIS BUILD SOLID BULLETPROOF SIMPLE SHIT."* Followed by the clearer architectural ask: list the existing node states, then strip the rendering to the minimum architecture a human can read at a glance. The bug class chain (glow square-clip → FX5 floor 0.15 → dim disks discarded at 0.15 → flicker during fade transitions) all traced back to ONE thing: **the glow halo is composited via alpha + a discard threshold, and the same threshold was killing every dim disk**. Phase 7 is the architectural reset: **DELETED the glow halo entirely**. **DELETED the discard threshold (0.15)**. **DELETED alpha-based dim** — dim is now COLOR-based (`dim_mult` multiplies RGB, not alpha; disks stay fully opaque, just darker). **DELETED the quad-scale headroom** (`selected_glow.w × 1.5`) — quad is 1.0× always, ONE shape per node. **REPLACED the glow** with a solid stroke ring drawn INSIDE the disk's outer edge in the same SDF fragment (no separate object, no separate alpha annulus, no composite math). **New SELECTED visual rule**: bigger disk (size_mult bumped 1.20 → 1.50) + a gold stroke band at the outer edge (width = `selected_stroke_width` = 0.12 of radius). The fragment shader is now ~12 lines of actual logic. **State table (4 declared, 1 transitional):** IDLE/FOCUSED → full family color at base size, no stroke; SELECTED → full color at 1.5× size with gold stroke ring; DIM → darker family color (RGB × 0.1 at default dim_amount_nodes=0.9) at base size, no stroke; TRANSITION → fill_rgb interpolates smoothly via state float, no pop-out at any threshold because nothing in the fragment is conditional on alpha crossing a value. PARAM_DEFAULTS cleanup: `selected_glow_strength` + `selected_glow_extent` DELETED. `selected_glow_color` RENAMED → `selected_stroke_color`. `selected_size_mult` bumped 1.20 → 1.50. Added `selected_stroke_width: 0.12`. View uniform binary layout unchanged (192 bytes); only the meaning of the trailing SELECTED slot changed from glow → stroke. Spec-lock header in forge.js rewritten with the Phase 7 reasoning. **Verified live in preview iframe**: zero console errors. Idle: 676 disks render solid with concentric-ring deity stencil on top. Lock Toyouke: focused 1-hop cluster shows solid disks; dim background visibly dim (darker, NOT discarded). **Bug class permanently closed**: no more discard threshold = no more disks vanishing during fade. No more glow annulus = no more square-clip class. No more alpha-based dim = no more disappearing dim disks. The node IS a colored circle with simple attributes; the shader reads like prose. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-7`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (NODE_SHADER rewritten + uniform writes + view struct field names), `src/js/views/forge.js` (PARAM_DEFAULTS + drawFrame call + spec-lock FX header), `index.html`. **Lesson**: when iterating doesn't converge after 3+ tries on the same bug class, **rip out the layer**, don't tweak its parameters. The glow halo was 2010-era visual decoration that cost us 5 sessions of cascading fixes. Phase 7 deletes it and the bug family along with it. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-6b-2026-05-20` — Phase 6B: GLYPH FOCUS-RULE — the actual architectural fix for "locked nodes transparent" — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. **John caught Phase 6A wasn't the fix** (*"WHY THEY ARE TRANSPARENT ?????? i keep hearing 'found a bug' BUT its that HARD to CHECK IF THERES ANY CODE PART THAT HIDES OR MAKES IT DISAPPEAR ?"*). Two screenshots showed the actual bug: (a) at mid-zoom, focused/hovered deities render as TORUS shapes — gold halo ring outside, dark/empty inside, with only a tiny concentric-ring deity glyph in the center. The disk between the halo and the glyph icon is gone. (b) Toyouke and other locked disks read as washed pastel because the deity-stencil atlas (a solid filled inner circle at radius 2.2 of 12-unit viewBox + an outer stroke ring at radius 4.6) overpaints the disk center at glyph_opacity × 0.85 ≈ 75% effective alpha. The "transparent inside" perception was the inner-fill of the deity stencil masking the disk's family color with the lightened tint. Phase 6A (screen-size fade) only kicked below 5 px — at the zoom John works at, the glyph was rendering at full opacity over the disk. **Phase 6B (the architectural fix)**: glyph fragment shader gains a focus rule. When focus is ACTIVE (any hover or lock), glyphs are HIDDEN on focused/selected disks (state=0) — those render clean solid + halo — and only show faintly on DIM background nodes (state=1) for at-a-glance type ID. In IDLE (no focus), glyphs paint on every disk at full opacity as before. **Implementation**: JS gates `dimAmountGlyphs` on `hasFocus` (drawFrame line ~1870) — 0 when idle, param value (0.9) when active. Fragment uses `is_active = step(0.01, dim_g); mult = mix(1.0, state * (1 - dim_g), is_active);`. Trace: idle s=0 → mult=1; active s=0 → mult=0 (HIDDEN); active s=1 → mult=0.1 (faint context). **Verified live**: (a) idle screenshot — all 676 deity disks show their concentric-ring deity stencil at full opacity (correct background reading). (b) lock Toyouke screenshot — Toyouke renders as a CLEAN SOLID PINK DISK with gold halo (no glyph wash); 1-hop neighbors Nisaba/Mama Sara/Changing Woman/Demeter all render as solid disks too; background dimmed disks. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-6b`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (GLYPH_SHADER fragment focus rule), `src/js/views/forge.js` (dimAmountGlyphs gated on hasFocus + spec-lock header). **Lesson**: when the user says "X is transparent," verify the disk fragment IS rendering — but ALSO check what OVERPAINTS it. The fragment passing the test doesn't mean the user SEES it — a later opaque overlay can hide it entirely. The `edgesAndNodesOnly()` toggle bisects this in one click. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-6a-2026-05-20` — Phase 6A: GLYPH SCREEN-SIZE FADE (partial fix, superseded by 6B same session) — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. After writing the handoff, kept going at John's request (*"you still have bandwidth to build? if so, go!"*). Diagnosed via debug fragment shader that painted disk-interior RED + glow-annulus GREEN: Toyouke's disk WAS rendering at full opacity at the center (red dot visible). The "transparent" perception was the **deity glyph atlas stencil covering ~85% of the small disk** in a `lightenColor(family_color, 0.55)` pastel tint, washing the disk's true red to pastel pink. Confirmed by toggling `edgesAndNodesOnly(true)` — Toyouke's disk shows as a bright solid red with the glyph pass off. **Fix (architectural, not numeric):** add a screen-size fade in the GLYPH_SHADER. Vertex shader now computes `screen_r = r * abs(view_scale.x) * viewport.x * 0.5` (the apparent on-screen radius in pixels) and passes it to fragment. Fragment multiplies the final alpha by `smoothstep(5.0, 10.0, screen_r)`. Below 5 px the glyph is fully faded out — the disk's family color reads solid and clean at fit-zoom (the overview where John works). Above 10 px (when the user zooms in to inspect a node) the glyph paints at full opacity, exposing the type symbol. Smooth blend in between. Zero per-frame JS work — all computation in GPU. **Verified live in preview iframe**: deities mode boots clean, FRAME 0.2 ms, Toyouke (locked from LS) renders as a solid pink disk + subtle gold halo — the pastel wash is gone. All other deities ALSO read as solid colored disks at fit-zoom. Reverted the diagnostic shader override (RED disk-interior / GREEN glow-annulus) + the temp `_forgeDebug.probeStateSlice` / `probeForceTick` surfaces that were used to confirm fade-pipeline correctness in the headless preview env (rAF is throttled in headless, so nodeStates wasn't converging without explicit ticking — confirmed tickNodeFades works correctly when invoked, ruling out fade-pipeline bugs as the cause). Updated the FX section of the spec-lock header in forge.js with the Phase 6A bullet. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-6a`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (GLYPH_SHADER vertex+fragment), `src/js/views/forge.js` (spec-lock comment), `index.html`. **Lesson saved**: when John reports "X looks transparent," the disk fragment shader is usually doing its job — the bug is upstream in compositing, not the SDF math. Use `edgesAndNodesOnly` to bisect disk-vs-glyph in one toggle before deep-diving the disk math. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-5c-2026-05-20` — Phase 5C of the layered Forge rebuild: node + glyph opacity UNIFIED on GPU — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Triggered by John's architectural critique mid-Phase-5B verification: *"This STILL is not a solid build, where the nodes is build with fancy or gimmicks with multiple objects hidding and not higgind — instead of a NODE that just transforms properties in SIMPLE TERMS AVOIDING BUGS"* + "getting a bit slow now outside hovering." Both symptoms traced to the SAME root cause: disk and glyph had two different opacity pipelines — disk opacity uniform-driven in GPU, glyph opacity computed CPU-side per frame in `refreshGlyphAlphas`. Two paths, one notional "node" — they could drift, and the JS loop was the slow-pan source. Fix: move glyph opacity to GPU using the SAME dim formula as the disk. **(View struct extended)** Repurposed the 8th unused `bucket_hot_colors` slot as `glyph_params: vec4<f32>` (xy = glyph_opacity + dim_amount_glyphs). EDGE_SHADER clamps to 0..6 now (only 7 real buckets). UBO size unchanged (192 bytes). **(GLYPH_SHADER fragment redesigned)** Now computes `a = tex.a × in.tint.a × glyph_op × dim_mult` where `dim_mult = mix(1.0, 1.0 - dim_amount_glyphs, in.state)` — exact mirror of NODE_SHADER's disk dim formula. State comes from `nodeStateVbo` (same buffer the disk reads). One opacity logic, one place, drift impossible by construction. **(`refreshGlyphAlphas` DELETED entirely)** The per-frame O(N) CPU loop is gone — it was the only thing setting alpha column values per frame. Now the alpha column is set ONCE to 1.0 at rebuild time and never touched again. The shader does the rest via uniforms + state. **(`local.glyphInstancesDirty` flag REMOVED)** No longer needed — state changes propagate via `nodeStateVbo` which is written every frame anyway. Removed the setters in `camera.onChange`, `recomputeFocus`, `tickNodeFades`, `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer`. Replaced with a simpler `local._glyphRebuildDirty` that's true only when `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer` reallocates (i.e., on mode-switch/rebake) — the static-VBO upload gate, not the per-frame state gate. **(Off-viewport cull removed)** The CPU cull I had was redundant — WebGPU's vertex-clip step culls off-viewport instances for free. The remaining cost (vertex shader running for off-screen instances) is microseconds at any plausible N. **What this fixes**: the visual inconsistency John reported (Toyouke/Ninurta/Mullissu rendering differently despite being same type+similar family) cannot recur — every glyph computes opacity from the same uniforms + the same per-instance state via the same formula. Slow-pan eliminated — `camera.onChange` no longer triggers per-frame JS work for glyphs. **Code reduction**: ~110 lines deleted (refreshGlyphAlphas function + all dirty-flag plumbing + the off-viewport cull math). **Verified live in preview iframe**: deities mode boots clean (676 nodes / 3033 edges / FRAME 0.1 ms / zero console errors); atlas still `{640, 512, 10 mip levels}`; locked Zeus from LS still hydrates + glow halo visible; focused 1-hop neighbors (Apollo, Athena, Hera, Hephaestus, etc.) light up with their glyphs intact; pan/zoom smooth. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-5c`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (refreshGlyphAlphas DELETED + dirty-flag plumbing simplified + drawFrame passes glyph uniforms), `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (View struct + GLYPH_SHADER + EDGE_SHADER clamp + drawFrame uniform write), `index.html` (cache-bust), `00_meta/ACTIVE-UX.md`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. **Lesson saved**: `feedback_audit_formulas_need_default_check.md` extended — this is the THIRD audit-recommended numeric threshold that regressed in one session (FX5 discard floor 0.05 vs 0.15; FX2 cull threshold 4 vs 2.55; and now this — though Phase 5C is architectural not numeric, it removes the entire class of CPU/GPU opacity drift). The pattern is consistent: trust empirical observation over audit-prescribed numbers; always check defaults; prefer architectural simplification over numeric tweaks when correctness keeps slipping.

> **`opus-forge-fx5-floor-fix-2026-05-20` — restore the 0.15 discard floor so the glow square-clip class stays closed — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Mid-session catch by John: the FX5 derived-discard formula (`0.05 + max(0, strength-1.0)*0.1`) assumed strength≥1.0 baseline; PARAM_DEFAULTS has selected_glow_strength=0.50, so the formula gave 0.05 — way LOWER than the empirically-proven 0.15. The whole bug class Phase 4B FX5 was meant to permanently close (square-clip artifact) returned immediately on Mastema, Mullissu, Fujin/Raijin. Fix: `0.15 + max(0.0, strength - 0.5) * 0.20` — keeps 0.15 floor at default, scales UP at higher strength (not below). At strength=0.5 → 0.15 (proven safe); at strength=1.0 → 0.25; at strength=2.0 → 0.45. Lesson saved as memory `feedback_audit_formulas_need_default_check.md`: goblin-proposed derivations assume a baseline; if the result at current PARAM_DEFAULTS is worse than the pre-fix constant, it's a regression in disguise. Every eager `device.createBuffer` / `createTexture` wrapped with `own()`. Lazy paths (`ensureBuffer`, `ensureDepthTex`, `setGlyphAtlas`) `disown(old) + own(new)` on grow / replace. `destroy()` iterates `owned[]` then `device.destroy()`. Closes T2.6 (atlasTex / glyphInstanceVbo / glyphUvUbo silent leak). Verified live: `ownedCount()` reports 12 active GPU resources at deities mount. **(N4) `packedAtScale` post-fit fix** — explicit `local.packedAtScale = camera.state.scale;` immediately after `packNodes` in `rebuildForMode`. Was previously only set in `rebakeNodes` (line 2135), saved at runtime only by the `||` fallback in `camera.onChange`. Verified: `dumpPackedAtScale()` returns `{packedAtScale: 0.05, currentScale: 0.05, ratio: 1.0000}`. Removes a latent bug that would reappear the moment anyone "cleans up" the fallback. **(N5) CROSS-PIPELINE INVARIANT comment** at `webgpu.js:1082` (the `nodeStateVbo` write site) — large boxed comment documenting that the glyph pass reads this same buffer as its third vertex buffer, and the contract: allocate-before-glyph-bind, write-before-glyph-pass, length ≥ max(node, glyph) × 8. Backward compatible (`np.tierFor || graph.buildTierClassifier(...)`). **(N7 polish) `_colorCache` comment** at `node.js:32` documenting the bounded ~100-entry module-scope cache + the "do not move into local" warning. **(N8 polish) `TIER_RADIUS` fallback comment** at `node.js:27` documenting it as legacy fallback for non-Forge callers; Forge always overrides via `PARAM_DEFAULTS` `node_radius_tier*`. **`_forgeDebug` helpers added**: `nodeOnly(on?)` toggle (zeros edge + glyph counts in drawFrame for visual isolation testing), `dumpHitGrid()` (cellSize / cols / rows / nonEmptyCells / maxBucketSize / totalEntries / avgPerNonEmpty), `dumpPackedAtScale()` (packedAtScale / currentScale / ratio), `countNodeVboWrites()` (proxies renderer's counter), `ownedCount()` (proxies renderer's owned list length). > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-0-2026-05-20` — Phase 0 of the layered Forge rebuild: dev panel DELETED entirely + foundation audit shipped — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. After John's call (*"at this stage is a mess, we must audit the component ground up — make a solid proper node, then behaviors, then wires, then fx, then management"* + *"we can remove the panel completely and REDO later if needed to avoid bugs if helpful"*), dispatched **5 parallel read-only audit goblins** mapping every drift class, scale risk, lifecycle invariant, animation pipeline issue, and GPU pipeline risk across the Forge view. Synthesized into [`AUDIT/forge-robustness-lock-plan-2026-05-20.md`](../AUDIT/forge-robustness-lock-plan-2026-05-20.md) + executed via [`AUDIT/forge-rebuild-layered-spec-2026-05-20.md`](../AUDIT/forge-rebuild-layered-spec-2026-05-20.md). **Headline findings of the 5 audits**: (1) pipeline is structurally correct (depth-direction fix verified, no shader bugs, no remaining wholesale-replace anti-patterns); (2) all residual perf lives in 5 mechanical fixes (hit-test grid, static-VBO dirty flag, glyph alpha settled-fade short-circuit, label DOM cap, glyph viewport+min-size cull); (3) three drift-class families still active (two `default:` tables, one-shot Option-B sync, missing destroy()-list entries) — Phase 0 kills the entire family by removing the dev panel; (4) "IDLE-hover feels heavier than LOCKED" is a UX-design call, not a perf bug — math: per-frame compute is identical between IDLE and LOCKED, what differs is visual mass (600 nodes vs 10); (5) two structural invariants are load-bearing but undocumented (`nodeStateVbo` cross-pipeline reuse + glyph-z=disk-z via draw-order). **Phase 0 (this commit)** deletes: `src/js/engine/dev-panel-forge.js` (670 lines), the `dev-panel-forge.js` + `icon-library.js` script tags in `index.html`, the entire `#forge-dev-tab` + `#forge-dev-panel` + `.fdp-*` CSS block in `src/styles/app.css` (~454 lines), the Option-B pull block + `setParam` / `setIcon` / `setFont` functions + `window._forge.setParam/setIcon/setFont/PARAM_DEFAULTS` bridge exports + related comments in `src/js/views/forge.js` (~155 lines). Plus minor comment cleanups in `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` + `src/js/engine/contract.js`. **Net**: forge.js 2361→2206 lines, app.css 6419→5935 lines, dev-panel-forge.js 670→0 lines = ~1300 lines of drift surface eliminated. **PARAM_DEFAULTS in forge.js is now the SINGLE source of truth for visual parameters** — no panel, no LS hydration, no `window.AtlasEngineForgeDevPanel`, no two-defaults divergence, no Option-B race. **Verified live in preview iframe**: Forge boots at `?view=forge`, wheel renders deities (676 nodes / 3033 edges), DEVICE active bgra8unorm, FRAME 0.1 ms, zero console errors, no `#forge-dev-panel` / `#forge-dev-tab` / `AtlasEngineForgeDevPanel` / `_forge.setParam` / `_forge.PARAM_DEFAULTS` in the page. > **`opus-forge-gpu-glyphs-depth-fix-plus-handoff` — depth-direction bug fix + comprehensive handoff doc for fresh agents — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's report after `286308b`: "better but not there yet, lots of nodes hover without the symbol just a bigger circle, log out for fresh agent." Root cause caught: the per-instance z values in `286308b` were BACKWARDS for the depth test. In WebGPU clip space, smaller z = closer to camera = wins less-equal depth test. Disk z values: selected=0.0, focused=0.3, dim=0.6. Glyph z values shipped at 0.01/0.10/0.50 — LARGER than parent disks → depth test rejected the glyph for selected nodes (0.01 > 0.0 → glyph hidden behind its own larger disk). Bug visible across MANY nodes because every hover briefly makes a node selected (selectedSet = lockedSet ∪ {hoverId}), so each hover registered the "bigger disk no glyph" flash. **Fix**: glyph z = parent disk z exactly. Glyphs draw AFTER disks so equal-z passes less-equal test, glyph wins via draw order. For overlapping instances at different z, depth test correctly hides dim glyph behind focused/selected disks — exactly what the old occlusion-zone hack approximated, now native. **Plus comprehensive handoff doc** at `AUDIT/forge-glyph-migration-handoff-2026-05-20.md` for fresh agents picking up Forge work. Covers: the 5 live GPU pipeline components, the 3 obsolete DOM paths (do-not-resurrect list), per-instance data layout, the full iteration log of what we tried + what broke + what worked, things to verify + likely-future-bugs, what's NOT yet done (glyph_tint not wired, icon overrides ignored, alpha-refresh unconditional, atlas at 64px not 128px), file map, debug API examples. Plus three lessons in the doc: (1) don't defer architectural fixes 5 times for "small fixes"; (2) don't trust memory of clip-space conventions (this commit fixes my flipped z direction); (3) don't conflate "fade broken" with "rendering broken" (most fade reports were downstream of DOM thrash). Cache-bust `20260520-gpu-glyphs-depth-fix`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (shader z mapping), `AUDIT/forge-glyph-migration-handoff-2026-05-20.md` (new), `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-gpu-glyphs-cleanup-v2` — three audit-fixes on top of the GPU glyph migration: glyph_scale, selected size_mult, per-instance z-layering — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's first-look feedback after the architectural fix (`1226559`): "substantially better!" but flagged three real visual regressions: (1) glyph sizes felt different from before — they were, the DOM glyph applied a `glyph_scale` multiplier (~0.85) that the GPU pack didn't, so GPU glyphs were ~18% bigger; (2) selected node's glyph appeared missing — selected disk grows by `selected_size_mult` (default ~1.5x) but the glyph stayed at base radius, so the larger disk covered it; (3) overlapping glyphs in dense areas looked chaotic — all GPU glyphs rendered at fixed z=0.02 so last-drawn won, no layering. Fixes shipped: **(a) glyph_scale applied** in `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer` — multiplies the packed radius by `local.params.glyph_scale`, matching prior DOM sizing. **(b) Per-instance (state, selected) bound** to the glyph pipeline as a third vertex buffer (`shaderLocation: 3, float32x2`) — REUSES the existing `nodeStateVbo` (1:1 with nodes, same order) so zero duplicate data, just an additional `setVertexBuffer(2, nodeStateVbo)` call in the glyph pass. **(c) Shader applies `selected_size_mult`** to the quad radius via the new instance attr — selected disk + glyph grow together. **(d) Per-instance z** computed in vertex shader from state/selected: selected anchor glyph at z=0.01 (in front of selected disks at 0.0), focused at z=0.10 (in front of focused disks at 0.3), dim at z=0.50 (in front of dim disks at 0.6). Overlapping glyphs now sort cleanly via depth test instead of last-draw-wins. Plus answered John's "is the fade causing slowness" question: no, per `frame: 0.1ms` measurement in the dump; the perceived slowness pre-GPU-migration was the 663-DOM-spans-rewritten-per-frame cliff which is now gone. The new GPU pipeline's per-frame alpha refresh + 21KB GPU buffer write is sub-ms even at full rate. Cache-bust `20260520-gpu-glyphs-cleanup`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (glyph shader + pipeline buffer binding + draw pass), `src/js/views/forge.js` (rebuild applies glyph_scale, refresh simplified), `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-glyphs-into-webgpu` — the architectural fix John kept telling me to stop deferring; glyphs moved from DOM (663 SVG spans) into the WebGPU canvas via a texture-atlas instanced quad pass — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John called out the circling pattern: *"WHY havent we done that , and we're here circling?"* He was right — every prior commit (occlusion-zone hack, label-pre-create, fade-aware rebakes, hover-coalesce, glyph culling) was a workaround for the DOM/canvas seam that this commit eliminates at the source. **What shipped:** (1) Atlas rasterizer in `src/js/engine/graph/glyph.js` — `buildAtlas(cellPx)` async, rasterizes all 17 type-glyph SVGs into a single Canvas2D image (4×5 grid at 64px cells) with currentColor → white substitution, returns canvas + per-type UV rects + typeToIdx Map. (2) New GLYPH_SHADER in WGSL — instanced textured quads, per-instance `(pos.xy, r, glyphIdx)` + `(tintRGB, alpha)`, samples atlas via uniform UV rects, multiplies by per-instance tint, z=0.02 (in front of all disk depth layers). (3) Renderer wires: shader module + UV uniform buffer + atlas texture (1×1 dummy until upload) + bilinear sampler + new bind-group layout (4 bindings) + new pipeline + per-frame glyph instance VBO + `setGlyphAtlas(canvas, uvRects)` method + glyph render pass at the end of `drawFrame`. (4) View layer: `buildAtlas(64)` fires async at engine boot → `setGlyphAtlas` upload; `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer()` at every `rebuildForMode` packs per-node `[pos.x, pos.y, r, idx, tint.r, tint.g, tint.b, baseAlpha]`; `refreshGlyphAlphas()` per frame reads `local.nodeStates` and updates the alpha column so glyphs fade with focus via the existing animation pipeline. (5) DOM glyph machinery FULLY retired — `glyphOverlay` div, `local.glyphEls` Array, `local.glyphFamilyColor` Map, `syncGlyphPositions` function, `syncGlyphFocus` function (with occlusion-zone hack), `rebakeGlyphsForMode` DOM loop, `.forge-glyph` + `.forge-glyphs-overlay` CSS rules all deleted or no-op'd. setParam routes glyph_opacity / glyph_scale / glyph_tint to `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer + drawFrame`. **Verified live**: 0 DOM glyphs in the page after reload, atlas uploaded, GPU pass renders at frame time ~0.1ms (vs prior DOM stall when zoomed out), no console errors. **The whole arc of fixes** documented in `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md` is now obsolete in the way it matters — there is no DOM/canvas seam to work around, no per-frame ~160k DOM writes to cull, no glyph occlusion zone to hack. Depth pipeline handles everything natively. Cache-bust `20260520-gpu-glyphs-only`. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/glyph.js`, `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-glyph-cull` — viewport + min-size culling on glyphs eliminates the zoom-out perf cliff John discovered — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's clinching observation: *"IF I zoom REALLY close, it animates well! this is a memory thing most likely."* Pinpointed the perf cliff exactly. Root cause: `syncGlyphPositions` runs every drawFrame (60Hz) and iterates ALL 663 glyphs (for deities mode), setting 4 style properties per glyph (left / top / width / height) = ~160k DOM writes per second. When zoomed IN, off-screen glyphs are composite-clipped by browser → small paint work. When zoomed OUT, ALL 663 SVG glyphs are simultaneously in viewport → browser paints all of them every frame → memory pressure → JS thread can't keep up with rAF → fade stutters. Two cheap culls shipped: **(1) Min-size cull** — glyphs computing to < 4px on screen get `display: none` (too small to convey info). At fit-to-extent in deities mode, 100% of glyphs are culled → zero glyph paint work. **(2) Viewport cull** — glyphs whose screen bbox falls outside `[0, vp.w] × [0, vp.h]` get `display: none`. At deep zoom, only the handful near cursor render. Both use `display: none` (cheaper than `visibility: hidden` because browser skips paint entirely) + state-cache check (only set display when state actually changes — avoids redundant DOM writes). Animation pipeline log doc (`AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md`) updated with discovery + fix as section #7. Cache-bust `20260520-glyph-cull`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-hover-coalesce-plus-pipeline-log` — coalesce hover via rAF + drop redundant drawFrame + hoist nodesById + comprehensive animation-pipeline log for future agents — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John flagged IDLE-vs-LOCKED hover asymmetry persists after the label-DOM-precreation fix, called it "architectural flaw, log everything for fresh agents". Three remaining audit recommendations shipped + comprehensive log doc. **(1) Coalesce `setHoverId` via rAF** — was firing `recomputeFocus` synchronously on every pointermove that crossed a node boundary (~120Hz on John's Mac, 120 full recomputes/sec, JS thread can't keep up with rAF, animation stutters). Now schedules ONE `recomputeFocus` per rAF max, light sync updates (cursor class + status text) stay immediate so cursor feedback is responsive. **(2) Drop redundant trailing `drawFrame()` in `recomputeFocus`** — `startAnimLoop` schedules `animTick` which calls `drawFrame` on first iteration, the extra `drawFrame` was one redundant GPU submit per call. **(3) Hoist `nodesById` Map to `local.mode.nodesById`** — built once in `rebuildForMode`, reused by scrubber filter in `recomputeFocus` (was allocating a fresh Map every hover). **Plus** `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md` — a comprehensive living doc covering: the full architecture (buffers, flow, invariants), every animation-related commit's iteration history (what we tried, what broke, what worked, why), things flagged but not yet fixed, things to NOT do, file map for fresh agents, glossary, debug-via-dumpBugState howto. Future agents touching the animation pipeline MUST read this first. **Remaining architectural note** (documented in the log): IDLE-hover inherently has a magnitude difference vs LOCKED-hover — IDLE→hover flips ~600 nodeStates values (mass dim), LOCKED→hover-change flips ~5-10. The fade animates equally in both, but IDLE looks "busier" because more changes. This is a UX design call (less aggressive dim on IDLE-hover?) rather than a bug. Cache-bust `20260520-hover-coalesce`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md` (new), `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-audit-fix-fade-flow-restored` — Plan-agent-audited proper fix for IDLE-hover lag + click-feels-buffered + LOCKED-fade-broken regression — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John flagged that the previous commit (`536f9cb`) broke LOCKED-hover smoothness AND made clicks feel "stuck" — the pre-warm approach was treating the wrong symptom. Dispatched a Plan agent to audit the animation pipeline; it surfaced 5 real bugs ranked by impact. Three highest-priority fixes shipped here. **(1) ROOT CAUSE of IDLE-hover slowness: label DOM materialization, not the fade pipeline.** First hover from settled IDLE created ~47 label divs on the spot (`appendChild` × 47 + a batched reflow) → render-thread stall for 1-2 frames → swallowed the fade visually. LOCKED-hover felt smooth because labels for the locked node ALREADY existed in DOM. Fix: pre-create label DOM for every node in the mode at `rebuildForMode` time (single batched DocumentFragment append for the whole mode). Labels start at opacity:0 (CSS default, no `data-visible`), zero perf cost until shown. First hover is now pure attribute flip — no DOM allocation, no reflow. Verified: 676 labels pre-created at mount in deities mode, 0 visible initially. **(2) `rebakeNodes` was bypassing the fade pipeline** — wholesale-replaced `local.nodeStates` with a fresh `interleavePairs()` result, killing any in-flight node fade by snapping current to target. So zoom drifts or dev-panel slider tweaks during a hover-transition caused the disk fade to JUMP instead of continuing. Mirrored the fade-aware pattern that `rebakeEdges` already used: update `nodeTargets` in place, only resize `nodeStates` when length actually changed. **(3) Pre-warm removed entirely.** With (1) and (2) shipped, the original IDLE-hover lag is gone at its source. Pre-warm was treating the symptom (delayed visual response) when the real cause was the DOM stall. Removed cleanly — fade animation now flows uninterrupted in both IDLE and LOCKED modes. **Audit also surfaced (deferred to follow-ups):** `recomputeFocus` not coalesced — pointermove + pointerup fire it back-to-back in <16ms; scrubber filter rebuilds `nodesById` per call (cheap-ish at 676 nodes, hurts at 10k); redundant trailing `drawFrame()` in `recomputeFocus` (one extra frame per call); `09_symbols/`/`06_themes/` queued renames still pending. Cache-bust `20260520-fade-flow-restored`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN. The audit doc is at `AUDIT/lens-expansion-audit-2026-05-19.md`... wait, that's the lens one — the animation audit lives in the conversation transcript, summarized in this entry.

> **`opus-forge-prewarm-fade-plus-scrubber-v2-plus-glow-bump` — pre-warm fade fixes IDLE-hover delay + 4-box scrubber + filter wiring + glow-clip threshold bump — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Five fixes. **(1) Pre-warm fade animation** — the real root cause of John's persistent IDLE-hover-slow report. The previous reflow-batch fix helped DOM perf but missed the actual issue: `drawFrame` at the end of `recomputeFocus` uses the CURRENT state buffer (pre-animation). animTick advances on the next rAF (~16ms later). For LOCKED-hover the state delta is tiny (most values barely change) so the gap is invisible. For IDLE→hover hundreds of node states flip from 0 to 1 (dim mass change) — the 16ms of "nothing happened" before animation begins reads as a delayed pop, not a smooth fade. Fix: after computing targets but before the first drawFrame, pre-advance both `local.nodeStates` and `local.edgeStates` ~30% toward target. The very first frame shows visible progress, the remaining ~70% completes via tickNodeFades / tickEdgeFades over the next ~7 frames. Hover response is now immediate. **(2) Glow-clip threshold bump** — John flagged Vairocana (and earlier Raijin) still showing the square outline despite commit `e52b3ea`. Root cause: when `selected_glow_strength` is high enough, glow alpha just inside the smoothstep upper bound is above the 0.08 discard threshold → writes depth at z=0.0 → adjacent disk fragments at z=0.3/0.6 fail less-equal test there → square-clip outline reappears. Bumped discard 0.08 → 0.15 in the WGSL node fragment shader. Combined with the existing 1.5× quad headroom, the entire glow tail that was depth-blocking neighbors is now discarded. Trade-off: very-outer glow pixels (alpha < 0.15) don't render — barely visible, far better than the square artifact. **(3) Search bar halved** per John's spec — was `flex: 1 1 320px; max-width: 360px;`, now `flex: 0 1 160px; max-width: 180px;`. **(4) Scrubber redesigned to 4 separate boxes** per John's spec: IN value box | slider | OUT value box | PRESENT value box. All four match the height of zoom-gizmo + search input. Boxes use same dark + mono + gold-accent styling; PRESENT box gets gold-tinted text to distinguish as playhead readout. Drag any of the 3 slider thumbs (IN/OUT/CENTER) and the relevant box updates live. **(5) Scrubber filter wired** — when user narrows IN/OUT (i.e., narrower than the full bounds), `recomputeFocus` now applies a date-range overlap check per node: if the node's existence period `[date_earliest..date_latest]` doesn't overlap `[inDate..outDate]`, its state target snaps to 1.0 (dim). Animates via the existing fade pipeline. Undated nodes stay visible. Probe-verified: dragging IN to 465 BCE correctly flagged 123 deities as out-of-range. **Plus dumpBugState extended** with `timeline`, `nodeTargets`, and `state.other` count fields for future fade-debug. Cache-bust `20260520-scrubber-debug-v2`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN. **Next natural targets**: ship Hulls per the integration plan (audit doc `forge-hulls-integration-plan-2026-05-20.md`); full motif-anchor authorship; goblins for the 7 still-empty 2026-05-18 lenses.

> **`opus-forge-timeline-scrubber-plus-label-reflow-fix` — bottom-bar timeline scrubber UI + label-reflow perf fix + Hulls integration plan — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Three deliveries. **(1) Timeline scrubber** in the bottom bar next to the search input. Three handles — IN (lower bound) + CENTER (playhead) + OUT (upper bound). 180px wide (half the search bar). Same dark + mono + gold-accent styling as zoom-gizmo / search. Range automatically derived from the current mode's nodes: scans every `date_earliest` / `date_latest` (plus YAML-raw fallbacks) and clamps to a sane archaeology window `[-15000, 3000]` so cosmogonic-motif nodes with `date_earliest = -1e9` don't squash the human-history span to a hairline. State on `local.timeline = {lo, hi, inDate, outDate, centerDate}`; readout shows `<in> · <center> · <out>` in BCE/CE format. Drag wired via track pointer-capture + per-handle data attribute. v1 ships the UI + range + readout + drag; filter wiring (dim out-of-range nodes via a separate per-instance attribute) is the natural next batch. **(2) Label-reflow perf fix** for John's "IDLE-hover slow + no fade in/out" report. Root cause: `syncLabels` previously called `el.getBoundingClientRect()` PER new label to force the layout-reflow that arms the CSS opacity transition. Hovering from idle creates ~47 new labels at once → 47 synchronous layouts per pointermove → layout thrash → fade animation stutters → perceived as "no animation, slow hovering". Fix: BATCH the reflow — collect all new labels, force ONE reflow via `document.body.offsetHeight`, then flip `data-visible` on all of them. Same fade-in guarantee, ~N× faster. The LOCKED-state hover felt smooth because lock-mode hovers reuse existing labels (no new DOM creation, no reflow needed). **(3) Hulls integration plan** at `AUDIT/forge-hulls-integration-plan-2026-05-20.md` per John's *"prepare to add the Hulls section, we should incorporate that, its the last big piece of the puzzle for the charts to have that styled decided"*. Plan covers: SVG-overlay approach (mirrors Pantheon V2's annular-wedge pattern), z-stack placement, 7-step implementation sequence (geometry helper → packer → SVG overlay → camera viewBox sync → focus/dim state → dev panel params → optional labels), rejected alternatives (WebGPU shader, Canvas2D), estimated ~2hr Lane B batch when greenlit. Also flagged (deferred): John noted some nodes (e.g., Raijin) still show the glow-clip square outline despite commit `e52b3ea` — likely a glow-strength × discard-threshold interaction; needs targeted shader-side investigation in a follow-up. Cache-bust `20260520-timeline-scrubber-v3` (16 occurrences). Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html`, `AUDIT/forge-hulls-integration-plan-2026-05-20.md` (new), `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.
